=== ./scripts/attack_simulation.sh ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash

# chmod +x scripts/attack_simulation.sh

# export UBUNTU_AGENT_IP=192.168.122.208
# export SSH_TEST_USER=soc-lab
# head -n 3 tests/wordlists/invalid-10.txt > /tmp/wazuh-invalid-prefix.txt
# ./scripts/attack_simulation.sh /tmp/wazuh-invalid-prefix.txt

set -Eeuo pipefail

TARGET="${UBUNTU_AGENT_IP:?Set UBUNTU_AGENT_IP first}"

TEST_USER="${SSH_TEST_USER:-soc-lab}"

WORDLIST="${1:?Usage: ./attack_simulation.sh WORDLIST}"

EXPECTED_VICTIM_IP="${EXPECTED_VICTIM_IP:-192.168.122.208}"

LAB_CIDR="${LAB_CIDR:-192.168.122.0/24}"


# Check required tools.

for tool in hydra python3; do

    if ! command -v "$tool" >/dev/null 2>&1; then

        echo "[ERROR] Missing dependency: $tool"
        exit 1

    fi

done


# Validate wordlist.

if [[ ! -f "$WORDLIST" || ! -r "$WORDLIST" ]]; then

    echo "[ERROR] Wordlist does not exist or is not readable."
    exit 1

fi


# Validate target IPv4 and restrict it to this lab.

python3 - "$TARGET" "$EXPECTED_VICTIM_IP" "$LAB_CIDR" <<'PY'

import ipaddress
import sys

target = ipaddress.ip_address(sys.argv[1])
expected = ipaddress.ip_address(sys.argv[2])
network = ipaddress.ip_network(sys.argv[3])

if target.version != 4:
    raise SystemExit("[ERROR] IPv4 only.")

if target != expected:
    raise SystemExit(
        f"[ERROR] Target must be the configured victim: {expected}"
    )

if target not in network:
    raise SystemExit(
        f"[ERROR] Target is outside lab network {network}"
    )

print(f"[PASS] Valid lab target: {target}")

PY


echo "[INFO] SSH brute-force simulation"
echo "[INFO] Target: $TARGET"
echo "[INFO] User: $TEST_USER"
echo "[INFO] Wordlist: $WORDLIST"

echo "[WARNING] Authorized isolated laboratory only."


hydra \
    -l "$TEST_USER" \
    -P "$WORDLIST" \
    -t 1 \
    -w 3 \
    "ssh://$TARGET"


echo "[INFO] Simulation finished."

=== ./scripts/inspect_firewall.sh ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash

# Read-only firewall inspection for Project 01.
# This script NEVER adds, deletes, flushes or changes firewall rules.

set -Eeuo pipefail

IP="${1:-192.168.122.112}"
LAB_CIDR="${LAB_CIDR:-192.168.122.0/24}"

python3 - "$IP" "$LAB_CIDR" <<'PY'
import ipaddress
import sys

try:
    ip = ipaddress.ip_address(sys.argv[1])
    network = ipaddress.ip_network(sys.argv[2], strict=False)
except ValueError as exc:
    raise SystemExit(f"[ERROR] Invalid IP or CIDR: {exc}")

if ip.version != 4:
    raise SystemExit("[ERROR] IPv4 only.")

if ip not in network:
    raise SystemExit(f"[ERROR] IP outside lab network: {network}")

print(f"[PASS] Inspecting lab IP: {ip}")
PY

echo
echo "===== IPv4 iptables rules mentioning $IP ====="

if ! command -v iptables-save >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "[INFO] iptables-save is unavailable."
else
    sudo iptables-save |
        awk -v ip="$IP" '
            index($0, ip) {
                print
                found=1
            }
            END {
                if (!found) print "[INFO] No matching IPv4 rule found."
            }
        '
fi

echo
echo "===== nftables rules mentioning $IP ====="

if ! command -v nft >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "[INFO] nft command is unavailable."
else
    sudo nft list ruleset |
        awk -v ip="$IP" '
            index($0, ip) {
                print
                found=1
            }
            END {
                if (!found) print "[INFO] No matching nftables line found."
            }
        '
fi

echo
echo "[INFO] Read-only inspection completed."
echo "[INFO] No firewall rules were changed."
echo "[INFO] Verify the Wazuh firewall-drop implementation before cleanup."

=== ./architecture/architecture.md ===
# Architecture â€” SSH Brute Force Detection & Active Response

## 1. Objective

Detect SSH brute-force attacks, password spraying, failed public-key
authentication and successful authentication following brute-force
activity. Apply automated IP containment through Wazuh Active Response
for the configured critical detection rules.

## 2. Lab Topology

| Host | Role | IP address |
|---|---|---|
| Kali | Authorized attacker / test host | `192.168.122.112` |
| my-agent | Ubuntu 24.04.5 LTS, Wazuh Agent | `192.168.122.208` |
| my-server | Ubuntu 24.04.5 LTS, Wazuh Manager | `192.168.122.178` |
| Fedora | Administration / trusted source | `192.168.122.1` as seen by Agent |

The Fedora address in the table is the source IP observed by the
Agent during SSH communication. The Fedora host's other observed
address (`172.16.62.231`) must not be substituted into the rule
without a new network verification.

## 3. Event and Detection Flow

```text
Kali (192.168.122.112)
        |
        | SSH authentication attempts
        v
my-agent (192.168.122.208)
        |
        | systemd journal / SSH events
        | Wazuh Agent -> TCP 1514
        v
my-server (192.168.122.178)
        |
        | Default SSH rules
        | Custom Project 01 rules
        v
Wazuh alerts / Dashboard
        |
        +--> 100001: Soft Detection
        +--> 100002: Brute Force
        +--> 100003: Public Key Failure
        +--> 100004: Password Spraying
        +--> 100005: Successful Login After Brute Force
        +--> 100010: Trusted IP
        |
        +--> Active Response for 100002 and 100005
                    |
                    v
              my-agent firewall
```

## 4. Log Collection

SSH events are collected from journald on `my-agent` and forwarded
to the Manager over TCP port 1514.

Do not configure `/var/log/auth.log` as a second source without
first checking whether it would duplicate journald events.

## 5. Rule Configuration

Source file in the repository:

`wazuh/rules/ssh_brute_force_rules.xml`

Deployed file on the Manager:

`/var/ossec/etc/rules/ssh_brute_force_rules.xml`

The Manager loads it through the configured `etc/rules` rule directory.
It is maintained separately from `local_rules.xml`.

## 6. Active Response

The Manager configuration contains separate Active Response
blocks for:

| Rule | Command | Location | Timeout |
|---|---|---|---:|
| 100002 | firewall-drop | Local Agent | 600 seconds |
| 100005 | firewall-drop | Local Agent | 600 seconds |

Rules 100001, 100003, 100004 and 100010 do not initiate
firewall-drop.

The trusted source `192.168.122.1` is excluded from the brute-force
and password-spraying detection and is included in the Manager's
Active Response whitelist.

Configuration validation does not prove that a live firewall block
has occurred. Live Active Response remains unverified until a
controlled test confirms the execution log and actual firewall state.

## 7. Validation Layers

1. XML syntax and Wazuh configuration validation.
2. Individual rule tests using `wazuh-logtest`.
3. Fast positive and negative regression tests.
4. Time-window boundary tests.
5. Controlled live SSH tests from Kali.
6. Active Response execution and firewall verification.

`wazuh-logtest` uses synthetic events. It does not simulate a live
SSH connection or prove actual firewall enforcement.

=== ./architecture/detection-flow.md ===
# Detection Flow â€” SSH Brute Force

## 1. Event Collection

The monitored endpoint is `my-agent` (`192.168.122.208`).

SSH authentication events are collected from `journald` by the
Wazuh Agent and forwarded to `my-server` (`192.168.122.178`)
over TCP/1514.

The deployment already collects SSH events through journald.
Do not add `/var/log/auth.log` as a duplicate input without
first verifying the actual collection configuration.

## 2. Default Wazuh SSH Rules

| Rule ID | Event type |
|---|---|
| 5715 | Successful SSH authentication |
| 5716 | SSH authentication failure / public-key failure parent |
| 5760 | Failed password / keyboard-interactive authentication |

The exact decoded fields must be checked against the event in
`wazuh-logtest`; do not assume every SSH failure has the same decoder
or base rule.

## 3. Custom Rule Evaluation

| Custom ID | Description | Level | Detection logic |
|---|---|---:|---|
| 100010 | Trusted IP | 3 | Base rule 5760 from `192.168.122.1` |
| 100001 | SSH Soft Detection | 6 | 3 matched 5760 failures from same source within 120 s |
| 100002 | SSH Brute Force | 10 | `frequency=4`, `timeframe=120`, same source IP |
| 100003 | Failed Public Key | 6 | Parent 5716 and `Failed publickey` match |
| 100004 | Password Spraying | 10 | 5 failed events, same source IP, different usernames within 300 s |
| 100005 | Successful Login After Brute Force | 14 | Successful login correlated with 100001 activity; same source IP and username within 300 s |

### Rule 100002 threshold behavior

The third failed-password event triggers 100001. In the verified
five-attempt sequence, the third event is no longer emitted as 5760
for subsequent custom-rule correlation.

Therefore, `100002` currently uses `frequency=4` and triggers on
the fifth attempt in the tested sequence.

### Trusted source handling

The trusted source address is `192.168.122.1`, the address observed
by the Agent for Fedora-originated SSH traffic.

Rule 100010 is evaluated for that source. It is excluded from
the brute-force and password-spraying rules.

## 4. Active Response

Manager configuration contains two separate response blocks:

| Trigger | Command | Location | Timeout |
|---|---|---|---:|
| 100002 | `firewall-drop` | `local` | 600 seconds |
| 100005 | `firewall-drop` | `local` | 600 seconds |

The response is intended to execute on the Agent associated with
the triggering alert.

No response is configured for 100001, 100003, 100004 or 100010.

Live enforcement remains unverified until the Agent execution log,
firewall state and a controlled connection attempt demonstrate
that the attacker IP was blocked.

## 5. Validation Layers

1. Parse XML files locally.
2. Validate the Manager rules and Active Response configuration.
3. Use `wazuh-logtest` for individual synthetic events.
4. Run the 17-case fast regression suite.
5. Run all 6 slow time-window tests with real delays.
6. Perform controlled SSH scenarios from Kali.
7. Verify Active Response and actual firewall behavior on the Agent.

`wazuh-logtest` validates rule matching only. It does not perform
network authentication, execute the response or change firewall state.

## 6. Related Files

- Rules: `wazuh/rules/ssh_brute_force_rules.xml`
- Active Response reference: `wazuh/configs/active-response.xml`
- Whitelist fragment: `wazuh/configs/global-whitelist.xml`
- Regression runner: `tests/regression/test_ssh_rules.py`
- Expected results: `tests/regression/expected-results.md`
- Actual results: `reports/test-results.md`
- Evidence register: `evidence/README.md`

=== ./wazuh/rules/ssh_brute_force_rules.xml ===
<group name="local,syslog,sshd,ssh_brute_force,">

  <!-- 100010: Trusted Fedora administrator -->
  <rule id="100010" level="3">
    <if_sid>5760</if_sid>
    <srcip>192.168.122.1</srcip>
    <description>Trusted IP: SSH authentication failure from $(srcip)</description>
    <group>authentication_failed,trusted_source,</group>
  </rule>

  <!-- 100001: SSH Soft Detection - 3 failures / 120 seconds -->
  <rule id="100001" level="6" frequency="3" timeframe="120">
    <if_matched_sid>5760</if_matched_sid>
    <same_srcip />
    <srcip negate="yes">192.168.122.1</srcip>
    <description>SSH Soft Detection: Multiple authentication failures from $(srcip)</description>
    <mitre>
      <id>T1110.001</id>
    </mitre>
    <group>authentication_failed,ssh_brute_force,</group>
  </rule>

  <!-- 100002: SSH Brute Force - 5 failures / 120 seconds -->
  <rule id="100002" level="10" frequency="4" timeframe="120">
    <if_matched_sid>5760</if_matched_sid>
    <same_srcip />
    <same_user />
    <srcip negate="yes">192.168.122.1</srcip>
    <description>SSH Brute Force: Confirmed attack from $(srcip)</description>
    <mitre>
      <id>T1110.001</id>
    </mitre>
    <group>authentication_failed,brute_force,ssh_brute_force,</group>
  </rule>

  <!-- 100003: SSH Public Key Failure -->
  <rule id="100003" level="6">
    <if_sid>5716</if_sid>
    <match>Failed publickey</match>
    <description>SSH Public Key Authentication Failure from $(srcip)</description>
    <mitre>
      <id>T1110.001</id>
    </mitre>
    <group>authentication_failed,publickey_failure,ssh,</group>
  </rule>

  <!-- 100004: SSH Password Spraying - 5 different users / 300 seconds -->
  <rule id="100004" level="10" frequency="4" timeframe="300">
    <if_matched_sid>5760</if_matched_sid>
    <same_srcip />
    <different_user />
    <srcip negate="yes">192.168.122.1</srcip>
    <description>SSH Password Spraying: Multiple accounts targeted from $(srcip)</description>
    <mitre>
      <id>T1110.003</id>
    </mitre>
    <group>authentication_failed,password_spraying,ssh,</group>
  </rule>

<!-- 100005: Successful Login After Brute Force -->
  <rule id="100005" level="14" frequency="3" timeframe="300">
    <if_sid>5715</if_sid>
    <if_matched_sid>5760</if_matched_sid>
    <same_srcip />
    <same_field>dstuser</same_field>
    <srcip negate="yes">192.168.122.1</srcip>

    <description>CRITICAL: Successful SSH login after brute force from $(srcip)</description>

    <mitre>
      <id>T1110.001</id>
      <id>T1078</id>
      <id>T1021.004</id>
    </mitre>

    <group>authentication_success,brute_force,possible_compromise,</group>
  </rule>

</group>

=== ./wazuh/configs/active-response.xml ===

<ossec_config>

  <!-- Project 01: SSH Brute Force -->
  <active-response>
    <disabled>no</disabled>
    <command>firewall-drop</command>
    <location>local</location>
    <rules_id>100002</rules_id>
    <timeout>600</timeout>
  </active-response>

  <!-- Project 01: Successful Login After Brute Force -->
  <active-response>
    <disabled>no</disabled>
    <command>firewall-drop</command>
    <location>local</location>
    <rules_id>100005</rules_id>
    <timeout>600</timeout>
  </active-response>

</ossec_config>

=== ./wazuh/configs/global-whitelist.xml ===
<!--
  Project 01 whitelist addition.
  Add this single entry inside the EXISTING whitelist-containing
  <global> section on the Manager.
  Do not replace that section or remove existing default entries.
-->
<white_list>192.168.122.1</white_list>

=== ./tests/regression/test_ssh_rules.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3

import argparse
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
LOGTEST = "/var/ossec/bin/wazuh-logtest"

CUSTOM_RULES = {
    100001, 100002, 100003,
    100004, 100005, 100010
}

RULE_LEVELS = {
    100001: 6,
    100002: 10,
    100003: 6,
    100004: 10,
    100005: 14,
    100010: 3,
}


def case(name, filename, exact=None, required_at=None,
         must_have=None, allowed=None, forbidden=None,
         delays=None, critical_last=False):

    return {
        "name": name,
        "file": ROOT / filename,
        "exact": exact,
        "required_at": required_at or {},
        "must_have": set(must_have or []),
        "allowed": set(allowed) if allowed is not None else None,
        "forbidden": set(forbidden or []),
        "delays": delays,
        "critical_last": critical_last,
    }


EMPTY = set()

FAST_CASES = [
    case("Two failed logins",
         "tests/negative/normal-failed-login.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY]),

    case("Three failed logins",
         "tests/positive/brute-force-3fails.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, {100001}]),

    case("Five failed logins",
         "tests/positive/brute-force-5fails.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, {100001}, EMPTY, {100002}]),

    case("Five failures followed by successful login",
         "tests/positive/brute-force-success-after-5fails.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, {100001}, EMPTY, {100002}, {100005}],
         must_have={100001, 100002, 100005},
         allowed={100001, 100002, 100005},
         critical_last=True),


    case("Ten failed logins",
         "tests/positive/brute-force-10fails.log",
         required_at={2: 100001, 4: 100002},
         must_have={100001, 100002},
         allowed={100001, 100002}),

    case("Three failures followed by success",
         "tests/positive/brute-force-success-after-3fails.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, {100001}, {100005}],
         must_have={100001, 100005},
         critical_last=True),

    case("Eight failures followed by success",
         "tests/positive/brute-force-success-after-8fails.log",
         required_at={2: 100001, 4: 100002},
         must_have={100001, 100002, 100005},
         allowed={100001, 100002, 100005},
         critical_last=True),

    case("Success by different user",
         "tests/negative/different-user-success.log",
         required_at={2: 100001},
         allowed={100001},
         forbidden={100005}),

    case("Success from different IP",
         "tests/negative/different-source-success.log",
         required_at={2: 100001},
         allowed={100001},
         forbidden={100005}),

    case("Mixed users from same IP",
         "tests/negative/mixed-users-failed-login.log",
         required_at={2: 100001},
         allowed={100001}),

    case("Mixed source IP addresses",
         "tests/negative/mixed-sources-failed-login.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY]),

    case("Trusted administrator",
         "tests/negative/trusted-admin-failed-login.log",
         exact=[{100010}, {100010}, {100010}]),

    case("Normal successful login",
         "tests/negative/normal-successful-login.log",
         exact=[EMPTY]),

    case("Public key failure",
         "tests/positive/publickey-failure.log",
         exact=[{100003}]),

    case("Two failures boundary",
         "tests/boundary/brute-force-2fails.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY]),

    case("Four failures boundary",
         "tests/boundary/brute-force-4fails.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, {100001}, EMPTY]),

    case("Success after only two failures",
         "tests/boundary/success-after-2-fails.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY]),
]


SLOW_CASES = [
    case("Five-user password spraying",
         "tests/positive/password-spray-5users.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY, {100004}],
         delays=[61, 61, 61, 61, 0]),

    case("Five failures outside 120 seconds",
         "tests/boundary/five-fails-outside-120s.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY],
         delays=[61, 61, 61, 61, 0]),

    case("Four-user spraying boundary",
         "tests/boundary/password-spray-4users.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY],
         delays=[61, 61, 61, 0]),

    case("One-user is not password spraying",
         "tests/boundary/password-spray-one-user.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY],
         delays=[61, 61, 61, 61, 0]),

    case("Slow failed logins outside 120 seconds",
         "tests/negative/slow-failed-login.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, EMPTY],
         delays=[61, 61, 0]),

    case("Success after 300 seconds",
         "tests/boundary/success-after-300s.log",
         exact=[EMPTY, EMPTY, {100001}, EMPTY],
         delays=[2, 2, 301, 0]),
]


def run_logtest(lines, delays):
    # Temporary files avoid stdout/stderr pipe-buffer deadlocks
    # when wazuh-logtest emits output for multiple events.
    with tempfile.TemporaryFile(mode="w+t") as stdout_file, \\
         tempfile.TemporaryFile(mode="w+t") as stderr_file:

        proc = subprocess.Popen(
            [LOGTEST],
            stdin=subprocess.PIPE,
            stdout=stdout_file,
            stderr=stderr_file,
            text=True,
        )

        try:
            for line, delay in zip(lines, delays):
                if proc.poll() is not None:
                    raise RuntimeError(
                        f"wazuh-logtest exited early: {proc.returncode}"
                    )

                if proc.stdin is None:
                    raise RuntimeError("wazuh-logtest stdin is unavailable")

                proc.stdin.write(line + "\\n")
                proc.stdin.flush()

                if delay:
                    time.sleep(delay)

            if proc.stdin is not None:
                proc.stdin.close()
                proc.stdin = None

            try:
                proc.wait(timeout=sum(delays) + 90)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                proc.kill()
                proc.wait()
                raise RuntimeError("wazuh-logtest timed out")

        except BaseException:
            if proc.poll() is None:
                proc.kill()
            proc.wait()
            raise

        stdout_file.seek(0)
        stderr_file.seek(0)

        stdout = stdout_file.read()
        stderr = stderr_file.read()

        if proc.returncode != 0:
            raise RuntimeError(stderr + "\\n" + stdout)

        return stdout + stderr


def parse_events(output):
    chunks = re.split(
        r"(?=\*\*Phase 1: Completed pre-decoding\.)",
        output
    )

    results = []

    for chunk in chunks:
        if "**Phase 1:" not in chunk:
            continue

        phase3 = re.search(
            r"\*\*Phase 3: Completed filtering \(rules\)\."
            r"(.*?)(?:\*\*Alert to be generated\.|\Z)",
            chunk,
            re.S
        )

        if not phase3:
            raise RuntimeError("Missing Phase 3 result")

        rule = re.search(
            r"^\s*id: '(\d+)'",
            phase3.group(1),
            re.M
        )
        level = re.search(
            r"^\s*level: '(\d+)'",
            phase3.group(1),
            re.M
        )

        if not rule or not level:
            raise RuntimeError("Cannot parse rule ID or level")

        results.append({
            "id": int(rule.group(1)),
            "level": int(level.group(1)),
        })

    return results


def run_case(test):
    path = test["file"]

    if not path.is_file():
        raise FileNotFoundError(path)

    lines = [
        line.strip()
        for line in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
        if line.strip()
    ]

    if not lines:
        raise ValueError(f"Empty test: {path}")

    delays = test["delays"] or [0] * len(lines)

    if len(delays) != len(lines):
        raise ValueError("Delay count does not match event count")

    events = parse_events(run_logtest(lines, delays))

    if len(events) != len(lines):
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"Expected {len(lines)} events, got {len(events)}"
        )

    actual = [
        {event["id"]} & CUSTOM_RULES
        for event in events
    ]

    found = set().union(*actual)
    errors = []

    if test["exact"] is not None:
        if actual != test["exact"]:
            errors.append(
                f"Per-event mismatch: expected {test['exact']}, "
                f"got {actual}"
            )

    for index, rid in test["required_at"].items():
        if index >= len(actual) or rid not in actual[index]:
            errors.append(
                f"Rule {rid} missing on event #{index + 1}"
            )

    missing = test["must_have"] - found

    if missing:
        errors.append(f"Missing rules: {sorted(missing)}")

    if test["allowed"] is not None:
        unexpected = found - test["allowed"]

        if unexpected:
            errors.append(
                f"Unexpected rules: {sorted(unexpected)}"
            )

    forbidden = found & test["forbidden"]

    if forbidden:
        errors.append(
            f"Forbidden rules detected: {sorted(forbidden)}"
        )

    critical_positions = [
        i for i, rules in enumerate(actual)
        if 100005 in rules
    ]

    if test["critical_last"]:
        if critical_positions != [len(events) - 1]:
            errors.append(
                "100005 must trigger exclusively on the last event"
            )

    for event in events:
        rid = event["id"]

        if rid in RULE_LEVELS and event["level"] != RULE_LEVELS[rid]:
            errors.append(
                f"Rule {rid}: expected level {RULE_LEVELS[rid]}, "
                f"got {event['level']}"
            )

    trace = ", ".join(
        f"#{i + 1}:{e['id']}/L{e['level']}"
        for i, e in enumerate(events)
    )

    if errors:
        print(f"[FAIL] {test['name']}")
        for error in errors:
            print("   ", error)
        print("   ", trace)
        return False

    print(f"[PASS] {test['name']}")
    print("   ", trace)
    return True


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument(
        "--include-slow",
        action="store_true",
        help="Run time-window tests with real delays"
    )
    args = parser.parse_args()

    if not Path(LOGTEST).is_file():
        print("[ERROR] Run on Wazuh Manager")
        return 2

    tests = FAST_CASES + (
        SLOW_CASES if args.include_slow else []
    )

    passed = 0
    failed = 0

    for test in tests:
        try:
            if run_case(test):
                passed += 1
            else:
                failed += 1
        except Exception as error:
            failed += 1
            print(f"[ERROR] {test['name']}: {error}")

    print("\n===== REGRESSION SUMMARY =====")
    print(f"Total:  {len(tests)}")
    print(f"PASS:   {passed}")
    print(f"FAIL:   {failed}")

    return 1 if failed else 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

# SSH Brute Force â€” Expected Regression Results

**Target release:** Wazuh 4.14.10-1  
**Manager:** `192.168.122.178`  
**Agent:** `192.168.122.208`  
**Attacker:** `192.168.122.112`  
**Trusted source:** `192.168.122.1`

This file describes expected behavior. It is not a record of
completed test execution. Actual results belong in
`reports/test-results.md`.

## Expected custom rule levels

| Rule | Level |
|---|---:|
| 100001 | 6 |
| 100002 | 10 |
| 100003 | 6 |
| 100004 | 10 |
| 100005 | 14 |
| 100010 | 3 |

## Positive scenarios

| Scenario | Expected custom rule |
|---|---|
| 3 failed passwords from one source | 100001 |
| 5 failed passwords within 120 s | 100001, 100002 |
| 10 failed passwords | 100001, 100002 |
| Failed public-key authentication | 100003 |
| 5 different usernames from one source within 300 s | 100004 |
| 3 failures followed by successful login | 100001, 100005 |
| 5 failures followed by successful login | 100001, 100002, 100005 |
| 8 failures followed by successful login | 100001, 100002, 100005 |
| Trusted source failed authentication | 100010 only |

## Five failures followed by successful login

Expected event-by-event results:

| Event | Expected rule | Level |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 5760 | 5 |
| 2 | 5760 | 5 |
| 3 | 100001 | 6 |
| 4 | 5760 | 5 |
| 5 | 100002 | 10 |
| 6 | 100005 | 14 |

The 100005 alert must appear on the successful authentication event.

## Negative and boundary scenarios

| Scenario | Expected custom rule behavior |
|---|---|
| 2 failed passwords | No custom rule |
| 4 failed passwords | 100001 only |
| 5 failures outside the 120-second window | No 100002 |
| 4 different usernames | No 100004 |
| 5 failures for one username | No 100004 |
| Mixed source IP addresses | No cross-source correlation |
| Success for a different username | No 100005 |
| Success from a different source IP | No 100005 |
| Success after 300 seconds | No 100005 |
| Trusted source failures | 100010 only |

## Regression inventory

The current runner defines:

- 17 fast cases.
- 6 slow cases requiring real delays.

The slow cases are:

1. Five-user password spraying.
2. Five failures outside 120 seconds.
3. Four-user spraying boundary.
4. One user is not password spraying.
5. Slow failed logins outside 120 seconds.
6. Success after 300 seconds.

The slow runner deliberately waits between events. Do not remove
the delays merely to shorten the test; doing so would invalidate
the time-window test.

## Active Response policy

| Rule | Command | Timeout |
|---|---|---:|
| 100002 | `firewall-drop` | 600 seconds |
| 100005 | `firewall-drop` | 600 seconds |

All other custom rules must not trigger automatic blocking.

A regression result does not prove Active Response execution.
Live firewall verification is documented separately.

=== ./evidence/README.md ===
# Project 01 â€” Evidence Register

**Project:** SSH Brute Force Detection & Active Response  
**Environment:** Wazuh 4.14.10-1  
**Last reviewed:** 2026-11-12

## Evidence policy

Only retain screenshots and sanitized logs captured from the
current lab deployment.

Old screenshots from earlier deployments have been archived outside
the repository. They must not be presented as evidence of the
current Wazuh 4.14.10 deployment.

Synthetic `wazuh-logtest` results must be distinguished from
live SSH and firewall tests.

Never store passwords, private keys, `client.keys`, API tokens,
or other authentication secrets in this directory.

## Required detection evidence

| File | Required evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `100001_soft.png` | Rule 100001, level 6 | NOT CAPTURED |
| `100002_brute_force.png` | Rule 100002, level 10 | NOT CAPTURED |
| `100003_publickey.png` | Rule 100003, level 6 | NOT CAPTURED |
| `100004_password_spraying.png` | Rule 100004, level 10 | NOT CAPTURED |
| `100005_critical.png` | Rule 100005, level 14 | NOT CAPTURED |
| `100010_trusted_ip.png` | Rule 100010, level 3 | NOT CAPTURED |

## Required Active Response evidence

| File | Required evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `active_responses_log.png` | Active Response execution on Agent | NOT CAPTURED |
| `ar_blocked_ip.png` | Evidence of the attacker IP block | NOT CAPTURED |
| `iptables_drop.png` | Exact firewall chain/rule responsible for block | NOT CAPTURED |

## Required test evidence

| File | Required evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `regression-summary.png` | Final regression totals and results | NOT CAPTURED |

## Evidence acceptance criteria

1. Record the current date and environment.
2. Show the relevant agent, rule ID, description and timestamp.
3. Sanitize unrelated usernames and sensitive information.
4. For Active Response, correlate the Manager alert with the
   Agent execution log and actual firewall state.
5. Do not mark Active Response PASS from configuration validation
   or `wazuh-logtest` alone.
6. Update this register immediately after capturing new evidence.
